1	# Build New Preview Workflow
2	
3	**Goal:** Turn user intent into a hardened, reviewable artifact.
4	
5	**CRITICAL:** If a step directs you to another snapshot file, read it fully and follow it. No exceptions.
6	
7	Subagents, when the capability is available, are an important part of this workflow. Use them as directed by the workflow steps.
8	If you need an explicit user instruction to run them, ask once now for the whole workflow run.
9	
10	## READY FOR DEVELOPMENT STANDARD
11	
12	A specification is "Ready for Development" when:
13	
14	- **Actionable**: Every task has a file path and specific action.
15	- **Logical**: Tasks ordered by dependency.
16	- **Testable**: All ACs use Given/When/Then.
17	- **Complete**: No placeholders or TBDs.
18	- **Sufficient**: No known requirement, acceptance, dependency, or implementation gaps remain unresolved.
19	- **Coherent**: No unresolved ambiguities or internal contradictions.
20	
21	## SCOPE STANDARD
22	
23	A specification should target a **single user-facing goal** within **900–1600 tokens**:
24	
25	- **Single goal**: One cohesive feature, even if it spans multiple layers/files. Multi-goal means >=2 **top-level independent shippable deliverables** — each could be reviewed, tested, and merged as a separate PR without breaking the others. Never count surface verbs, "and" conjunctions, or noun phrases. Never split cross-layer implementation details inside one user goal.
26	  - Split: "add dark mode toggle AND refactor auth to JWT AND build admin dashboard"
27	  - Don't split: "add validation and display errors" / "support drag-and-drop AND paste AND retry"
28	- **900–1600 tokens**: Optimal range for LLM consumption. Below 900 risks ambiguity; above 1600 risks context-rot in implementation agents.
29	- **Neither limit is a gate.** Both are proposals with user override.
30	
31	## Conventions
32	
33	- Every operational cross-file reference in this workflow is an absolute snapshot path. Open it directly; do not resolve it relative to a skill directory.
34	- `{project-root}`-prefixed paths resolve from the project working directory.
35	- Whenever this workflow captures or records a version-control revision, obtain the full canonical identifier directly from version control and preserve it verbatim.
36

# STATUS DEFINITIONS:
# ==================
# Epic Status:
#   - backlog: Epic not yet started
#   - in-progress: Epic actively being worked on
#   - done: All stories in epic completed

# Story Status:
#   - backlog: Story only exists in epic file
#   - ready-for-dev: Story file created, ready for development
#   - in-progress: Developer actively working on implementation
#   - review: Implementation complete, ready for review
#   - done: Story completed

# Retrospective Status:
#   - optional: Can be completed but not required
#   - done: Retrospective has been completed

# Action Item Status:
#   - open: Committed during a retrospective, not yet addressed
#   - in-progress: Actively being worked on
#   - done: Completed

# WORKFLOW NOTES:
# ===============
# - Epic transitions to 'in-progress' automatically when its first story starts (via build's sprint sync)
# - Stories can be worked in parallel if team capacity allows
# - Developer typically creates the next story after the previous one is 'done' to incorporate learnings
# - Dev moves story to 'review', then runs code-review (fresh context, different LLM recommended)
# - Retrospective appends its action items to action_items; the status view surfaces open ones

# Epic 1 Context: Find a Park

<!-- Compiled from planning artifacts. Edit freely. Regenerate with compile-epic-context if planning docs change. -->

## Goal

Deliver a small, keyless web app (a 2-hour take-home) that lets a resident find a local park in the NYC-area sample set on a map or in a list and open its details, on phone or desktop, by mouse, touch, or keyboard, with missing data handled honestly. The interviewer must be able to run it with one install and one start command and judge it from the README. A modest app that works and is well explained beats an ambitious one with broken core features; stop at 2 hours and document what is unfinished.

## Stories

- Story 1.1: Scaffold app and park data layer
- Story 1.2: Park list with keyboard selection
- Story 1.3: Park details with missing-data handling
- Story 1.4: Map of parks synced with selection
- Story 1.5: Responsive and accessibility polish with design applied
- Story 1.6: Tests and submission README

## Requirements & Constraints

- **Cross-cutting (every story):** no geolocation ever requested; no credentials or API keys; never invent missing data; `parks.sample.json` is never edited (copied into the app byte-for-byte); keyboard focus always visible.
- The list plus details view must be a complete alternative to the map. All 12 parks appear in the list, including ones with missing fields; parks without valid lat/lng get no marker but stay in the list and details.
- Every one of the 12 parks renders; null/missing/empty fields are omitted or labelled, never crash the UI. Failed images degrade without a broken-image icon.
- A data-load failure shows an error message with retry, never a blank screen.
- At 375px and desktop widths, list → details → back works with no horizontal scroll.
- Unredacted AI transcripts (Claude Code, exported by the developer before each fresh story context, and Claude Design) and design files are part of the submission; the README must say where they live.
- Non-goals: backend, accounts, deployment, data editing. Search, filters, current location only if core is done with time to spare.
- Where the design and the spec disagree, the spec wins.

## Technical Decisions

- **Stack:** React + Vite, Tailwind CSS (mobile-first), `react-leaflet` + Leaflet with OpenStreetMap standard tiles (keyless, attribution visible), Vitest + React Testing Library. No component library.
- **Data loading (readiness decision):** the spec says static import, but loading/error/retry states require a mechanism that can actually fail. Use one that can (e.g. `fetch` from `public/` or a dynamic `import()`) and document the choice in the README.
- **Routing (readiness decision):** React Router. `/` is the list, `/parks/:id` is details (supersedes the design's `?park=` option). Unknown id shows "park not found" with a way back. Browser Back must work.
- **Data shape:** array of park objects with `id`, `name` (treat as required); `description`, `location.{lat,lng,address}`, `amenities` (kebab-case tokens), `hours` (free text, shown as written), `images` (URL array), `acreage`, `rating` (0–5) may each be missing, null, or empty. Read nested fields null-safely (`park.location?.address`).
- **Normalization:** one null-safe module applies safe defaults with no fabricated values, sorts parks A–Z and numbers them 1–N; that number is shared by list cards and map markers. Centralize fallback rules in a small helper (e.g. `display(value, fallback)`).
- **Image URL transformation:** pixel art moves from `design/park-images/` to `src/assets/park-images/`. At load time each image URL maps by filename stem to the bundled asset (`.../prospect-1.jpg` → `park-images/prospect-1.png`); unmatched URLs pass through unchanged so `onError` fallback still applies. Do not mutate source data. Document in README.
- **Amenity labels:** replace `-` with a space and capitalize the first letter (`dog-run` → "Dog run"); unknown tokens are humanized, never dropped.
- **Map:** initial view fits bounds of parks with coordinates. Leaflet default marker icons need explicit asset imports under Vite (or use `L.divIcon` with a `<button>` inside). Markers are focusable, named "N. Park name"; the selected marker is amber and larger.
- **Tailwind tokens:** paper `#F6F4EE`, surface `#FFFFFF`, line `#DAD6CB`, ink `#1B2620`, muted `#4F5B54`, forest `#1E5B3F` / dark `#123826`, tag bg `#E9EFE7` / fg `#23472F`, amber `#B45309`, missing bg `#F0EDE5` / line `#C9C4B6`. Font: Public Sans (Google Fonts, 400–800). Focus ring: `focus-visible:outline focus-visible:outline-[3px] focus-visible:outline-amber focus-visible:outline-offset-2`.
- **Tests must cover** every known data gap (see UX section), a failing image, an unmatched image URL, the URL transformation without mutation, and the keyboard list → details → back flow.

## UX & Interaction Patterns

- **Landmarks:** `<header>`, `<main>`, `<aside aria-label="Parks">`, map region labelled "Map of parks". One `<h1>` per view; no clickable `<div>`s; icon-only buttons have `aria-label`s; touch targets ≥ 44×44px; text contrast ≥ 4.5:1.
- **Desktop (≥ 768px):** green header (app name "Find a Park", subtitle, park count); ~400px independently scrolling sidebar; map fills the rest. Selecting a park switches the sidebar to details, starting with an "All parks" back control.
- **Phone (< 768px):** single column with a List / Map toggle of two `<button>`s using `aria-pressed`, List default. Tapping a marker shows a preview card ("Park N of 12", name, address, hours, "View park details", close button "Close park preview"). Details take the full screen with an "All parks" back link.
- **Focus management:** opening details moves focus to the details heading; returning to the list moves focus to the originating card.
- **Park card:** single `<button>`/link per card (one Tab stop) in a `<ul>`; number badge, name (17px bold), address, first three amenities as tags plus "+N more".
- **Details order:** back control, photo area ("Photo 1 of N", alt text naming the park), name as `<h1>` (26–28px, 800), address, "Open in OpenStreetMap" link from lat/lng, `<dl>` fact tiles (Hours, Size in acres, Rating "4.7 / 5"), About, all amenities as checked tags.
- **Missing-data copy:** address → "Address not listed" (muted); description null → "No description has been provided for this park."; rating null → "Not yet rated" (never 0/empty stars); empty or failed images → dashed box "No photos for this park yet"; hours/acreage → "Not listed"; empty amenities → hide section or "No amenities listed"; no lat/lng → no marker, no OSM link. Missing fact tiles use the muted style (missing bg, dashed border, italic), as on the phone screen; the desktop mock's normal-style "Not yet rated" tile is a mistake.
- **Sample gaps:** Old Mill Botanical Garden (null description), Cedar Hill Nature Preserve (empty images, null rating; coordinates look like a placeholder, note in Known issues), East Ridge Trailhead (empty images), Highland Dog Park (missing address).
- **Loading/error states:** "Loading parks…" and "Couldn't load parks. Try again." with a retry button, using the card style.
- Visual reference: `design/screens/` (1280×800 and 390×844). `design/source/*.dc.html` is reference only, not app code.

## Cross-Story Dependencies

- 1.1 provides the data layer, routing, layout shell, tokens, and bundled images that all later stories build on.
- 1.2 needs the 1.3 back control for its focus-return-to-card criterion. **Readiness decision:** in 1.2, verify focus return using browser Back; 1.3 confirms it via the "All parks" control.
- 1.4 relies on the shared A–Z numbering from 1.1 and the selected-park route to highlight markers and open details.
- 1.5 is the full responsive/accessibility pass over 1.2–1.4 (which ship baseline accessibility).
- 1.6 tests cover behaviour from 1.1–1.3; the README records decisions from all stories, including the data-load mechanism and React Router choice.

<frozen-after-approval reason="human-owned intent — do not modify unless human renegotiates">

## Intent

**Problem:** `/parks/:id` shows only the park name (`ParkDetailsPlaceholder`). Residents can't see hours, size, rating, description, photos or amenities, or tell when a value is missing rather than wrong (CAP-3).

**Approach:** Replace the placeholder with a `ParkDetails` view in this order: an "All parks" back link, the photo area, the name as `<h1>`, the address, an "Open in OpenStreetMap" link, a `<dl>` of Hours/Size/Rating tiles, About, and Amenities. Every missing value gets the labelled fallback from `design/README.md`. Focus moves to the `<h1>` when details open.

## Boundaries & Constraints

**Always:** render the normalized park exactly as given and never invent a value; fallback copy matches the design table word for word; missing-value tiles use `bg-missing-bg`, a dashed `border-missing-line` and italic text; `focus-ring` and ≥ 44px targets on the back link and the OSM link; reuse `amenityLabel` from `src/data/format.js`; keep the `NotFound` branch and the `onOpen` id recording.

**Never:** a photo gallery or carousel (first photo only); map, markers or marker highlight (1.4); the desktop sidebar/phone header layout split or the List/Map toggle (1.5); an "Open now" badge or hours parsing; star icons; clickable `<div>`s; editing `parks.sample.json` or the normalizer.

**Decisions (agent-made, user-invisible or design-permitted):** empty amenities shows the Amenities heading with "No amenities listed" rather than hiding it. Rating shows one decimal (`4` → "4.0 / 5"). Size shows "N acres" ("1 acre" when N is 1). Photo alt is "Photo of {name}". The back control is a `<Link to="/">`, so the existing `lastParkId` mechanism returns focus to the card.

## I/O & Edge-Case Matrix

| Scenario | Input / State | Expected Output / Behavior | Error Handling |
|----------|--------------|---------------------------|----------------|
| Full data | Prospect Park | all sections; "Photo 1 of 2"; tiles "6:00 AM - 1:00 AM", "526 acres", "4.7 / 5"; 7 checked amenity tags | N/A |
| Missing address | Highland Dog Park | "Address not listed" in `text-muted` | N/A |
| Null description | Old Mill Botanical Garden | About reads "No description has been provided for this park." | N/A |
| Null rating | Cedar Hill | Rating tile "Not yet rated", muted style; no "0" | N/A |
| No images | Cedar Hill, East Ridge | dashed "No photos for this park yet" box, no `<img>` | N/A |
| Image fails | `<img>` fires `error` | the no-photo box replaces it | no broken-image icon |
| Missing hours / acreage | synthetic park | the tile reads "Not listed", muted style | N/A |
| Empty amenities | synthetic `amenities: []` | "No amenities listed" | N/A |
| No coordinates | synthetic `hasCoords: false` | no OSM link | N/A |
| Unknown amenity | `wifi`, `dog-run` | "Wifi", "Dog run" | humanized, never dropped |
| Back control | click "All parks" | `/` renders; that park's card has focus | N/A |

</frozen-after-approval>

## Code Map

- `src/App.jsx` -- `ParkDetailsPlaceholder` (find park by `:id`, call `onOpen`, `NotFound` fallback) is the code to replace. Move that lookup into the new component, or wrap it. `linkClass`/`cardClass` are reusable styles. `lastParkId` + `ParkList focusParkId` already restore card focus on any return to `/`.
- `src/data/parks.js` -- normalized park `{ id, name, number, description, address, lat, lng, hasCoords, amenities, hours, images, acreage, rating }`. Missing values are `null`/`[]`, `rating` is in 0–5 or `null`, and `images` already resolve to bundled assets. Don't change it.
- `src/data/format.js` -- `amenityLabel`; add the small display helpers here (the epic asks for a central `display(value, fallback)`-style rule set).
- `src/index.css` -- tokens `missing-bg`, `missing-line`, `tag-bg`, `tag-fg`, `muted`, `forest`, `line`, `surface`; the `focus-ring` utility.
- `src/App.test.jsx` -- the `renderAt(path)`, `okResponse()` (structuredClone of the sample, so tests can mutate it for synthetic parks) and `TestNav` patterns.
- `design/screens/02-desktop-park-selected.png`, `06-phone-details.png`, `07-phone-missing-data.png` -- the visuals: bordered "‹ All parks" button, a rounded photo with a dark "Photo 1 of N" pill at bottom-right, a 3-column tile grid, ✓ tags.
- OSM URL: `https://www.openstreetmap.org/?mlat={lat}&mlon={lng}#map=16/{lat}/{lng}`.

## Tasks & Acceptance

**Execution:**
- [x] `src/data/format.js` -- add `formatRating(n)` → "4.7 / 5" or `null`, `formatAcres(n)` → "526 acres" / "1 acre" or `null`, `osmUrl(lat, lng)`, and `display(value, fallback)` → `{ text, missing }` so the view applies every fallback through one rule.
- [x] `src/components/ParkDetails.jsx` -- `ParkDetails({ park })` renders the sections in order: `PhotoArea` (keyed by park id, `onError` → no-photo box), an `<h1 tabIndex={-1}>` focused on mount, a `<dl>` of `FactTile`s (muted variant when the value is missing), About, and Amenities as a `<ul>` of ✓ tags.
- [x] `src/App.jsx` -- the `/parks/:id` route renders `ParkDetails` for a found park (keep `onOpen` and `NotFound`); delete `ParkDetailsPlaceholder`.
- [x] `src/data/format.test.js`, `src/App.test.jsx` -- unit-test the new helpers; test every matrix row (synthetic rows by editing the cloned sample in a custom fetch mock), section order, and focus on the heading after opening from the list.

**Acceptance Criteria:**
- Given the list, when the user opens a park by keyboard, then the details `<h1>` has focus and is the only `<h1>` in the view.
- Given details, when the user activates "All parks", then the list renders and focus is on that park's card.
- Given a park with images, then one `<img>` shows with alt naming the park and a "Photo 1 of N" label.
- Given any of the 12 sample parks, when details render, then nothing crashes and no value reads "null", "undefined" or "NaN".

**Rejected**

- `false` — Spec `status: 'done'` vs sprint-status `review` (BH, AA): the fix would edit the spec under review, and this review's status sync settles it.
- `false` — Sprint status leaves 1-2 `in-progress` (BH): this was set deliberately by `6430967` after 1-2's review left patches open. This change did not cause it.
- `false` — Implementation Notes / Spec Change Log are empty (BH): the fix would edit the spec under review. The applied patches show in the diff and the triage log.
- `false` — `osmUrl` could emit `NaN` in the href (BH, EC, AA): the normalizer sets `hasCoords` only when `lat`/`lng` pass `numberInRange`, so the link never renders with non-finite coordinates.
- `low` — Null `name` gives an empty `<h1>` and alt "Photo of null", which the no-null test misses (EC, BH, AA): all 12 sample parks have names, `name` is required by the data contract, and the fix adds a fallback branch. This is the same call as 1.1 EC2, 1.2 BH1 and 1.3 pass-1 EC1/EC4.
- `low` — `0` acres / `0.0 / 5` rendered as real values (BH): the normalizer treats 0 as valid, no sample park has 0, and the design's "do not show 0" applies only to `null`.
- `false` — No range check on rating (BH): `normalizePark` already nulls ratings outside 0–5.
- `false` — Every address is muted, so the missing-address test is weak (BH): the design table asks only for "secondary text colour", and the design shows every address muted. This is the same call as pass-1 BH2.
- `false` — Deep link to `/parks/:id` moves focus to the `<h1>` (BH): focusing the page heading on route load is the intended SPA pattern, so nothing bad happens.
- `false` — The deferred-work entry lacks an id/status (BH): the ledger is new, and the loop sweep's `--migrate` handles pre-DW-format entries.
- `false` — "Photo 1 of N" implies a gallery (BH): `design/README.md` asks for exactly this label on a single image.
- `low` — A dash-only amenity token (`"-"`) renders an empty tag (EC): no sample data does this, and the fix adds a filter.
- `false` — Same park id re-rendered with new images keeps a stale failed state (EC): parks load once, and no path changes a park's images in place.
- `low` — `formatAcres` has no rounding or thousands separator (EC): sample acreages are integers ≤ 640. This is the same call as pass-1 EC3/BH4.
- `low` — `toFixed(1)` rounds a two-decimal rating (AA): no sample rating has two decimals, and one decimal is a spec Decision.
- `false` — Park-to-park refocus and `PhotoArea` reset are untested (AA, VG other): this is already deferred in `deferred-work.md` (VG2), so it is not a new finding.

## Design Notes

Focus-to-heading uses a ref plus `useEffect` keyed on `park.id`, so moving from one park to another also refocuses. The heading needs `tabIndex={-1}` to be focusable from code, and it gets no `focus-ring`, since a programmatic focus target is not a Tab stop. `PhotoArea` keeps a `failed` state and is keyed by park id, so a failure on one park does not hide another park's photo.

Missing-value tile golden example:

```jsx
<div className={missing ? 'border-dashed border-missing-line bg-missing-bg' : 'border-line bg-surface'}>
  <dt className="text-sm text-muted">Rating</dt>
  <dd className={missing ? 'italic text-muted' : 'font-semibold'}>{value ?? fallback}</dd>
</div>
```

## Verification

**Commands:**
- `npm test` -- expected: all tests pass.
- `npm run build` -- expected: succeeds with no errors.

**Manual checks (if no CLI):**
- `npm run dev`. Open Cedar Hill: dashed photo box and a muted "Not yet rated" tile. Open Prospect Park: the photo shows "Photo 1 of 2". Tab to "All parks", press Enter, and focus lands on the card.
======
---
title: 'Story 1.4: Map of parks synced with selection'
type: 'feature'
created: '2026-10-14'
baseline_commit: '529d0bc194228df30a91143d27d7c21b56c2b484'
status: 'done'
route: 'dispatch'
review_loop_iteration: 0
context:
  - '{project-root}/_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/epic-1-context.md'
---

<frozen-after-approval reason="human-owned intent — do not modify unless human renegotiates">

## Intent

**Problem:** The "Map of parks" region is a "Map coming soon." placeholder. Residents cannot see where the parks are or pick one by location.

**Approach:** Render a react-leaflet map with OpenStreetMap tiles, fitted to the parks that have coordinates. Each such park gets a numbered, focusable marker that uses the loader's `number`. The park on `/parks/:id` has an amber, larger marker. At ≥ 768px, activating a marker navigates to `/parks/:id`. At < 768px it opens a preview card instead.

## Boundaries & Constraints

**Always:** use loader `number`/order unchanged; `hasCoords` decides which parks get markers; visible OSM attribution; one Tab stop per marker, a real `<button>`, name "N. Park name", `focus-ring`; touch targets ≥ 44px; preview copy shows a missing address as "Address not listed" and missing hours as "Hours not listed"; no API keys.

**Never:** geolocation (`navigator.geolocation`, `locate()`); a desktop two-column sidebar, a phone List/Map toggle, or any layout restyling (Story 1.5); details content (Story 1.3); panning the map on selection; editing `parks.sample.json`.

## I/O & Edge-Case Matrix

| Scenario | Input / State | Expected Output / Behavior | Error Handling |
|----------|--------------|---------------------------|----------------|
| Happy path | 12 sample parks at `/` | 12 marker buttons named "1. …" to "12. …", none amber | N/A |
| Selected | `/parks/prospect-park` | Prospect Park's marker amber and larger; all others forest | N/A |
| No coords | a park with `hasCoords: false` | no marker; still in the list | N/A |
| Desktop activate | ≥ 768px, click or Enter on a marker | URL `/parks/:id`, park heading in aside | N/A |
| Phone tap | < 768px, tap marker 8 | preview: "Park 8 of 12", name, address, hours, "View park details", "Close park preview"; marker 8 amber; URL unchanged | N/A |
| Preview missing address | < 768px, Highland Dog Park | "Address not listed" (muted) | N/A |
| Preview → details | press "View park details" | navigates to `/parks/:id` | N/A |
| Preview close | press "Close park preview" | preview gone; focus back on that marker | N/A |
| Not loaded | loading or error | map region shows no map | N/A |

</frozen-after-approval>

## Code Map

- `src/App.jsx` -- shell; the `<section aria-label="Map of parks">` placeholder is replaced. The map renders outside `<Routes>`, so App reads the selected id with `useMatch('/parks/:id')`. Keep `ParksPanel`, `ParkDetailsRoute`, `lastParkId`, the landmarks and the loading/error states as they are.
- `src/data/parks.js` -- park fields used: `id`, `name`, `number`, `address`, `hours`, `lat`, `lng`, `hasCoords`. Do not change it.
- `src/index.css` -- tokens `forest`, `amber`, `surface`, `muted`, `line`, and the `focus-ring` utility; Tailwind classes inside divIcon HTML strings are picked up only if the literal class names appear in a scanned source file.
- `src/App.test.jsx` -- `renderAt`, `okResponse`, and `TestNav`; extend these. jsdom has no `matchMedia`, so stub it per test.
- `design/screens/02-desktop-park-selected.png`, `05-phone-map-selected.png` -- marker and preview visuals: white-ringed forest number circles (selected: amber, ~1.3×); a white card at the bottom of the map with an amber "Park N of 12", forest full-width button and round close "×".
- Versions: `leaflet@^1.9.4`, `react-leaflet@^5.0.0` (peer React 19).

## Tasks & Acceptance

**Execution:**
- [x] `package.json` -- add `leaflet`, `react-leaflet`.
- [x] `src/data/useIsDesktop.js` -- `useIsDesktop()`: `matchMedia('(min-width: 768px)')` with a change listener. If `matchMedia` is missing, return `true`.
- [x] `src/components/ParkMap.jsx` -- `ParkMap({ parks, selectedId, isDesktop, onActivate })`: `MapContainer` with `bounds` from the parks that have coordinates (fixed NYC centre if none), a `TileLayer` for OSM standard tiles with its attribution, and one `Marker` per park with `L.divIcon` HTML `<button type="button" class="… focus-ring">` holding an `aria-hidden` number and an `sr-only` "N. Name". Set `keyboard: false` on the marker so the button is the only Tab stop. Selected markers are amber, larger and have a higher `zIndexOffset`. Import `leaflet/dist/leaflet.css`.
- [x] `src/components/ParkPreview.jsx` -- the preview card. Its name is an `<h2>`. It has a "View park details" `<Link>` and an `aria-label="Close park preview"` button. On mount, focus moves to the card. On close, focus returns to the marker button.
- [x] `src/App.jsx` -- wire it up: `selectedId` comes from the route. Keep `previewId` in state, used only below 768px, and clear it on navigation or when switching to desktop. The highlighted id is `selectedId ?? previewId`. Activation navigates on desktop and sets `previewId` on phone. Give the map section an explicit height so Leaflet can render. Render no map before `ready`.
- [x] `src/components/ParkMap.test.jsx`, `src/App.test.jsx` -- test every matrix row, that Tab reaches each marker button, that the attribution text is present, and that `navigator.geolocation` is never called.

**Acceptance Criteria:**
- Given the app loads, when the map renders, then OSM tiles and the "OpenStreetMap" attribution show, the view fits the coordinate bounds, and no location is requested.
- Given a keyboard user, when they Tab into the map, then each marker button receives focus once, with the amber `focus-ring`.
- Given a production build, when markers render, then no broken default marker icon appears (divIcon only).

## Implementation Notes

- Marker focus return after "Close park preview" is driven by a `focusId`/`onFocused` pair on `ParkMap`: closing changes the highlight, react-leaflet then swaps the marker's divIcon (replacing its DOM), so the map focuses the new button in an effect that runs after the markers' own effects. Icons are memoised per park and state so unrelated renders never replace marker DOM.
- `ParkMap` accepts `isDesktop` per the contract but renders identically at every width; App decides what activation does.
- The preview is a `<section tabIndex=-1 aria-labelledby>` overlaid at the bottom of the map (`z-[1001]`, above Leaflet controls; `bottom-8` keeps the attribution visible). Escape also closes it.
- Map wrapper is `relative isolate h-[480px]` inside the section; the section lost `flex-1` (layout is otherwise unchanged; 1.5 restyles).
- Existing details tests used `document.querySelector('img')`; OSM tile `<img>`s now exist, so those queries are scoped to the details `<article>`.

## Spec Change Log

## Design Notes

**Marker as divIcon `<button>`:** a native button gives the name and Enter/Space for free, and Leaflet fires the marker `click` from the bubbling DOM click. `keyboard: false` keeps the wrapper div out of the Tab order. No default icon means no broken Vite icon paths.

**Layout before 1.5:** the map stays stacked under the aside at every width. Story 1.5 adds the sidebar and the List/Map toggle. Phone vs desktop behaviour is decided by viewport, not by layout, so 1.5 only restyles.

**Story 1.3 is merged:** `/parks/:id` renders `ParkDetailsRoute` → `ParkDetails`, which focuses its h1 on mount. A desktop marker activation therefore lands focus on the details heading.

## Verification

**Commands:**
- `npm test` -- expected: all tests pass.
- `npm run build` -- expected: succeeds with no errors.

**Manual checks (if no CLI):**
- `npm run dev`: tiles, attribution and 12 markers show; desktop click opens details with an amber marker; at 375px a tap shows the preview; no location prompt.

> **Canonical contract.** This SPEC and the files in `companions:` are the complete, preservation-validated contract for what to build, test, and validate. Source documents listed in frontmatter are for traceability — consult them only if you need narrative rationale or prose color this contract intentionally omits.

# Find a Park

## Why

A mandate to meet: a 2-hour take-home brief (Granicus Lighthouse) to build a small web app that helps residents find local parks. Evaluation weighs usefulness and accessibility for residents, clear communication of decisions, maintainable code that handles missing data, accountable AI use, and evidence of review and verification. A modest app that works and is well explained beats an ambitious one with broken core features.

## Capabilities

- **CAP-1**
  - **intent:** A resident sees where parks are on a map and can get from a marker to that park's details.
  - **success:** Every park with valid lat/lng has a marker; activating a marker opens the park's details or a clear link to them.
- **CAP-2**
  - **intent:** A resident browses all parks in a list and selects one without using the map.
  - **success:** All parks in the data appear, including ones with missing fields; a list item opens details by mouse, touch, and keyboard (Tab to it, Enter to open).
- **CAP-3**
  - **intent:** A resident views the available information for a park: name, address, description, amenities, hours, images, acreage, rating.
  - **success:** Each of the 12 sample parks renders; null, missing, or empty fields are omitted or labelled as not listed, never invented; broken image URLs degrade without a broken-image icon or crash.
- **CAP-4**
  - **intent:** The main tasks (find a park, open its details) work on phone and desktop, by keyboard, and with assistive technology.
  - **success:** At 375px width and at desktop width the list→details flow works with no horizontal scroll; every interactive element shows a visible focus indicator; regions and controls have readable text and clear labels.
- **CAP-5**
  - **intent:** The interviewer can run, understand, and judge the submission from its README.
  - **success:** README contains run instructions, what works, what was left out, decisions, assumptions, data/schema changes, known issues, how the result was checked, approximate time spent, and next steps before public use.

## Constraints

- Hard 2-hour box for the whole submission, including testing and README; stop at 2 hours and document what is unfinished.
- Must work without the resident's location.
- No private credentials in the repo; services must be keyless or document how the interviewer supplies a key.
- Data fields may be missing or null and arrays empty; the UI must not crash or fabricate details.
- The list and details view must be a complete alternative to map markers.
- Use the supplied `parks.sample.json`; document any extension of data or schema in the README.
- Unredacted AI conversation logs from every tool used are part of the submission: the developer manually exports the Claude Code transcript before each fresh story context, and exports the Claude Design transcript; the generated designs are committed in `design/`.
- Visual design follows `design/README.md` and `design/screens/`; where they conflict with this SPEC, this SPEC wins.

## Non-goals

- Backend, accounts, hosted deployment.
- Search, filters, current-location support — only if the core is done with time remaining.
- Editing or contributing park data.

## Success signal

- On a phone-width viewport and on desktop, a resident can open any of the 12 sample parks from the list using only the keyboard, and from a map marker by click/tap, and see its details with missing fields handled cleanly; the interviewer runs it from the README with one install and one start command.

## Assumptions

- Municipality is the NYC-area set implied by the sample coordinates; the app is titled generically ("Find a Park").
- Parks without valid lat/lng still appear in the list and details, just without a marker.
- A data-load failure shows an error message, not a blank screen.
# Stack

All choices confirmed by the user.

| Concern | Choice | Why |
|---|---|---|
| UI | React | User decision. |
| Build tool | Vite | Fast scaffold; one `npm install` + `npm run dev`. |
| Styling | Tailwind CSS, mobile-first | User decision; responsive by default. |
| Map | `react-leaflet` + Leaflet | User decision; no API key. |
| Tiles | OpenStreetMap standard tiles, with attribution | Keyless; satisfies the no-credentials constraint. |
| Data | Static import of `parks.sample.json` (copied into the app) | No backend. |
| Routing | Path-based `/parks/:id` for park details; `/` is the list | Linkable details, working Back. Supersedes the `?park=` option in `design/README.md`. |
| Tests | Vitest + React Testing Library | Cover every known gap in `park-data.md` (null description, empty images, null rating, missing address, failing image URLs) plus the keyboard list→details flow. |

## Conventions

- Map initial view fits the bounds of parks that have coordinates; never depends on geolocation.
- Leaflet's default marker icons need explicit asset imports under Vite, or markers render as broken images.
- Visual design, tokens, missing-data display copy, and accessibility rules come from `design/README.md` (adopted companion) and `design/screens/`.
- Park images: pixel art lives in `src/assets/park-images/` (moved from `design/park-images/`) and is resolved via the load-time URL transformation in `park-data.md`; the image-failure fallback is still required.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
merge_session_transcripts.py

Collect the main transcript and every subagent transcript for a Claude Code
session ID, then interleave them into one timeline sorted by timestamp.

Claude Code stores sessions under ~/.claude/projects/<project-slug>/:
    <session-id>.jsonl                       main conversation
    <session-id>/subagents/agent-*.jsonl     each subagent's full transcript
    <session-id>/**/*.jsonl                  any other agent transcripts (picked up too)

Usage:
    python3 merge_session_transcripts.py <session-id>
    python3 merge_session_transcripts.py <session-id> -f md -o session.md
    python3 merge_session_transcripts.py <session-id> -f jsonl -o merged.jsonl
    python3 merge_session_transcripts.py <session-id> --projects-dir /path/to/.claude/projects

Formats:
    text   readable plain text (default)
    md     Markdown, one heading per message
    jsonl  the original JSON entries, merged, each tagged with "_source"
"""

import argparse
import json
import sys
from datetime import datetime, timezone

// jsdom has no matchMedia: stub it per test. `set` simulates a viewport change.
function stubViewport(desktop) {
  const listeners = new Set()
  const mql = {
    matches: desktop,
    media: '(min-width: 768px)',
    addEventListener: (_type, fn) => listeners.add(fn),
    removeEventListener: (_type, fn) => listeners.delete(fn),
  }
  window.matchMedia = vi.fn(() => mql)
  return {
    set(value) {
      mql.matches = value
      act(() => listeners.forEach((fn) => fn()))
    },
  }
}

afterEach(() => {
  delete window.matchMedia
})

  const location = useLocation()

}

# Park data

Source: `requirements/parks.sample.json` — an array of 12 park objects.

## Shape

| Field | Type | May be missing/null/empty |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | string (slug) | treat as required |
| `name` | string | treat as required |
| `description` | string | yes |
| `location.lat`, `location.lng` | number | yes (no marker if absent) |
| `location.address` | string | yes |
| `amenities` | string[] (kebab-case tokens, e.g. `dog-run`) | yes / empty |
| `hours` | free-text string (e.g. `Dawn to dusk`, `24 hours`) | yes |
| `images` | string[] URLs | yes / empty |
| `acreage` | number | yes |
| `rating` | number (0–5) | yes |

Amenity tokens display as readable labels (`dog-run` → "Dog run"); unknown tokens fall back to the token humanized, never dropped.

## Image URL transformation

`parks.sample.json` is never edited. At load time each image URL is mapped by filename stem to a bundled asset: `https://images.example.com/prospect-1.jpg` → `src/assets/park-images/prospect-1.png` (source art: `design/park-images/`). A URL with no matching asset passes through unchanged, so the `onError` fallback still applies. All 12 sample URLs have a match. Describe this transformation in the README.

## Known gaps in the sample (test fixtures)

| Park | Gap |
|---|---|
| `old-mill-botanical-garden` | `description: null` |
| `cedar-hill-nature-preserve` | `images: []`, `rating: null` |
| `east-ridge-trailhead` | `images: []` |
| `highland-dog-park` | `location.address` missing |
| all | image URLs are `images.example.com` placeholders that do not resolve; the transformation above maps them to bundled art, and an unmapped URL must still fall back cleanly |

import { useCallback, useEffect, useState } from 'react'
import { loadParks } from './parks.js'

/** Loads parks once; `retry` refetches after an error. */
export function useParks() {
  const [attempt, setAttempt] = useState(0)
  const [state, setState] = useState({ status: 'loading', parks: [] })

  useEffect(() => {
    let cancelled = false
    setState({ status: 'loading', parks: [] })
    loadParks().then(
      (parks) => {
        if (!cancelled) setState({ status: 'ready', parks })
      },
      () => {
        if (!cancelled) setState({ status: 'error', parks: [] })
      },
    )
    return () => {
      cancelled = true
    }
  }, [attempt])

  const retry = useCallback(() => setAttempt((n) => n + 1), [])

  return { status: state.status, parks: state.parks, retry }
}

# Epic 1 Context: Find a Park

<!-- Compiled from planning artifacts. Edit freely. Regenerate with compile-epic-context if planning docs change. -->

## Goal

Deliver a small, keyless web app (a 2-hour take-home) that lets a resident find a local park in the NYC-area sample set on a map or in a list and open its details, on phone or desktop, by mouse, touch, or keyboard, with missing data handled honestly. The interviewer must be able to run it with one install and one start command and judge it from the README. A modest app that works and is well explained beats an ambitious one with broken core features; stop at 2 hours and document what is unfinished.

## Stories

- Story 1.1: Scaffold app and park data layer
- Story 1.2: Park list with keyboard selection
- Story 1.3: Park details with missing-data handling
- Story 1.4: Map of parks synced with selection
- Story 1.5: Responsive and accessibility polish with design applied
- Story 1.6: Tests and submission README

## Requirements & Constraints

- **Cross-cutting (every story):** no geolocation ever requested; no credentials or API keys; never invent missing data; `parks.sample.json` is never edited (copied into the app byte-for-byte); keyboard focus always visible.
- The list plus details view must be a complete alternative to the map. All 12 parks appear in the list, including ones with missing fields; parks without valid lat/lng get no marker but stay in the list and details.
- Every one of the 12 parks renders; null/missing/empty fields are omitted or labelled, never crash the UI. Failed images degrade without a broken-image icon.
- A data-load failure shows an error message with retry, never a blank screen.
- At 375px and desktop widths, list → details → back works with no horizontal scroll.
- Unredacted AI transcripts (Claude Code, exported by the developer before each fresh story context, and Claude Design) and design files are part of the submission; the README must say where they live.
- Non-goals: backend, accounts, deployment, data editing. Search, filters, current location only if core is done with time to spare.
- Where the design and the spec disagree, the spec wins.

## Technical Decisions

- **Stack:** React + Vite, Tailwind CSS (mobile-first), `react-leaflet` + Leaflet with OpenStreetMap standard tiles (keyless, attribution visible), Vitest + React Testing Library. No component library.
- **Data loading (readiness decision):** the spec says static import, but loading/error/retry states require a mechanism that can actually fail. Use one that can (e.g. `fetch` from `public/` or a dynamic `import()`) and document the choice in the README.
- **Routing (readiness decision):** React Router. `/` is the list, `/parks/:id` is details (supersedes the design's `?park=` option). Unknown id shows "park not found" with a way back. Browser Back must work.
- **Data shape:** array of park objects with `id`, `name` (treat as required); `description`, `location.{lat,lng,address}`, `amenities` (kebab-case tokens), `hours` (free text, shown as written), `images` (URL array), `acreage`, `rating` (0–5) may each be missing, null, or empty. Read nested fields null-safely (`park.location?.address`).
- **Normalization:** one null-safe module applies safe defaults with no fabricated values, sorts parks A–Z and numbers them 1–N; that number is shared by list cards and map markers. Centralize fallback rules in a small helper (e.g. `display(value, fallback)`).
- **Image URL transformation:** pixel art moves from `design/park-images/` to `src/assets/park-images/`. At load time each image URL maps by filename stem to the bundled asset (`.../prospect-1.jpg` → `park-images/prospect-1.png`); unmatched URLs pass through unchanged so `onError` fallback still applies. Do not mutate source data. Document in README.
- **Amenity labels:** replace `-` with a space and capitalize the first letter (`dog-run` → "Dog run"); unknown tokens are humanized, never dropped.
- **Map:** initial view fits bounds of parks with coordinates. Leaflet default marker icons need explicit asset imports under Vite (or use `L.divIcon` with a `<button>` inside). Markers are focusable, named "N. Park name"; the selected marker is amber and larger.
- **Tailwind tokens:** paper `#F6F4EE`, surface `#FFFFFF`, line `#DAD6CB`, ink `#1B2620`, muted `#4F5B54`, forest `#1E5B3F` / dark `#123826`, tag bg `#E9EFE7` / fg `#23472F`, amber `#B45309`, missing bg `#F0EDE5` / line `#C9C4B6`. Font: Public Sans (Google Fonts, 400–800). Focus ring: `focus-visible:outline focus-visible:outline-[3px] focus-visible:outline-amber focus-visible:outline-offset-2`.
- **Tests must cover** every known data gap (see UX section), a failing image, an unmatched image URL, the URL transformation without mutation, and the keyboard list → details → back flow.

## UX & Interaction Patterns

- **Landmarks:** `<header>`, `<main>`, `<aside aria-label="Parks">`, map region labelled "Map of parks". One `<h1>` per view; no clickable `<div>`s; icon-only buttons have `aria-label`s; touch targets ≥ 44×44px; text contrast ≥ 4.5:1.
- **Desktop (≥ 768px):** green header (app name "Find a Park", subtitle, park count); ~400px independently scrolling sidebar; map fills the rest. Selecting a park switches the sidebar to details, starting with an "All parks" back control.
- **Phone (< 768px):** single column with a List / Map toggle of two `<button>`s using `aria-pressed`, List default. Tapping a marker shows a preview card ("Park N of 12", name, address, hours, "View park details", close button "Close park preview"). Details take the full screen with an "All parks" back link.
- **Focus management:** opening details moves focus to the details heading; returning to the list moves focus to the originating card.
- **Park card:** single `<button>`/link per card (one Tab stop) in a `<ul>`; number badge, name (17px bold), address, first three amenities as tags plus "+N more".
- **Details order:** back control, photo area ("Photo 1 of N", alt text naming the park), name as `<h1>` (26–28px, 800), address, "Open in OpenStreetMap" link from lat/lng, `<dl>` fact tiles (Hours, Size in acres, Rating "4.7 / 5"), About, all amenities as checked tags.
- **Missing-data copy:** address → "Address not listed" (muted); description null → "No description has been provided for this park."; rating null → "Not yet rated" (never 0/empty stars); empty or failed images → dashed box "No photos for this park yet"; hours/acreage → "Not listed"; empty amenities → hide section or "No amenities listed"; no lat/lng → no marker, no OSM link. Missing fact tiles use the muted style (missing bg, dashed border, italic), as on the phone screen; the desktop mock's normal-style "Not yet rated" tile is a mistake.
- **Sample gaps:** Old Mill Botanical Garden (null description), Cedar Hill Nature Preserve (empty images, null rating; coordinates look like a placeholder, note in Known issues), East Ridge Trailhead (empty images), Highland Dog Park (missing address).
- **Loading/error states:** "Loading parks…" and "Couldn't load parks. Try again." with a retry button, using the card style.
- Visual reference: `design/screens/` (1280×800 and 390×844). `design/source/*.dc.html` is reference only, not app code.

## Cross-Story Dependencies

- 1.1 provides the data layer, routing, layout shell, tokens, and bundled images that all later stories build on.
- 1.2 needs the 1.3 back control for its focus-return-to-card criterion. **Readiness decision:** in 1.2, verify focus return using browser Back; 1.3 confirms it via the "All parks" control.
- 1.4 relies on the shared A–Z numbering from 1.1 and the selected-park route to highlight markers and open details.
- 1.5 is the full responsive/accessibility pass over 1.2–1.4 (which ship baseline accessibility).
- 1.6 tests cover behaviour from 1.1–1.3; the README records decisions from all stories, including the data-load mechanism and React Router choice.

142	async function findCards() {
143	  const list = await screen.findByRole('list')
144	  return within(list).getAllByRole('listitem')
145	}

147	function cardFor(name) {
148	  return screen
149	    .getAllByRole('listitem')
150	    .find((li) => within(li).queryByText(name, { exact: true }) !== null)
151	}

335	function stubData(mutate) {
336	  const data = structuredClone(sample)
337	  mutate?.(data)
338	  vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue({ ok: true, json: async () => data }))
339	}

341	async function openDetails(id, mutate) {
342	  stubData(mutate)
343	  renderAt(`/parks/${id}`)
344	  return screen.findByRole('heading', { level: 1 })
345	}
346	
347	function tile(label) {
348	  const dt = screen.getByText(label, { selector: 'dt' })
349	  return { box: dt.parentElement, value: dt.nextElementSibling }
350	}
351	
352	function expectMissingTile(label, text) {
353	  const { box, value } = tile(label)
354	  expect(value).toHaveTextContent(text)
355	  expect(value).toHaveClass('italic', 'text-muted')
356	  expect(box).toHaveClass('border-dashed', 'border-missing-line', 'bg-missing-bg')
357	}

359	function amenityLabels() {
360	  const list = screen.getByRole('list', { name: 'Amenities' })
361	  return within(list)
362	    .getAllByRole('listitem')
363	    .map((li) => li.textContent)
364	}

553	// jsdom has no matchMedia: stub it per test. `set` simulates a viewport change.
554	function stubViewport(desktop) {
555	  const listeners = new Set()
556	  const mql = {
557	    matches: desktop,
558	    media: '(min-width: 768px)',
559	    addEventListener: (_type, fn) => listeners.add(fn),
560	    removeEventListener: (_type, fn) => listeners.delete(fn),
561	  }
562	  window.matchMedia = vi.fn(() => mql)
563	  return {
564	    set(value) {
565	      mql.matches = value
566	      act(() => listeners.forEach((fn) => fn()))
567	    },
568	  }
569	}
570	
571	afterEach(() => {
572	  delete window.matchMedia
573	})
574	
575	function LocationDisplay() {
576	  const location = useLocation()
577	  return <p data-testid="location">{location.pathname}</p>
578	}
579	
580	function renderWithLocation(path) {
581	  return render(
582	    <MemoryRouter initialEntries={[path]}>
583	      <LocationDisplay />
584	      <App />
585	    </MemoryRouter>,
586	  )
587	}

> **Canonical contract.** This SPEC and the files in `companions:` are the complete, preservation-validated contract for what to build, test, and validate. Source documents listed in frontmatter are for traceability — consult them only if you need narrative rationale or prose color this contract intentionally omits.

# Find a Park

## Why

A mandate to meet: a 2-hour take-home brief (Granicus Lighthouse) to build a small web app that helps residents find local parks. Evaluation weighs usefulness and accessibility for residents, clear communication of decisions, maintainable code that handles missing data, accountable AI use, and evidence of review and verification. A modest app that works and is well explained beats an ambitious one with broken core features.

## Capabilities

- **CAP-1**
  - **intent:** A resident sees where parks are on a map and can get from a marker to that park's details.
  - **success:** Every park with valid lat/lng has a marker; activating a marker opens the park's details or a clear link to them.
- **CAP-2**
  - **intent:** A resident browses all parks in a list and selects one without using the map.
  - **success:** All parks in the data appear, including ones with missing fields; a list item opens details by mouse, touch, and keyboard (Tab to it, Enter to open).
- **CAP-3**
  - **intent:** A resident views the available information for a park: name, address, description, amenities, hours, images, acreage, rating.
  - **success:** Each of the 12 sample parks renders; null, missing, or empty fields are omitted or labelled as not listed, never invented; broken image URLs degrade without a broken-image icon or crash.
- **CAP-4**
  - **intent:** The main tasks (find a park, open its details) work on phone and desktop, by keyboard, and with assistive technology.
  - **success:** At 375px width and at desktop width the list→details flow works with no horizontal scroll; every interactive element shows a visible focus indicator; regions and controls have readable text and clear labels.
- **CAP-5**
  - **intent:** The interviewer can run, understand, and judge the submission from its README.
  - **success:** README contains run instructions, what works, what was left out, decisions, assumptions, data/schema changes, known issues, how the result was checked, approximate time spent, and next steps before public use.

## Constraints

- Hard 2-hour box for the whole submission, including testing and README; stop at 2 hours and document what is unfinished.
- Must work without the resident's location.
- No private credentials in the repo; services must be keyless or document how the interviewer supplies a key.
- Data fields may be missing or null and arrays empty; the UI must not crash or fabricate details.
- The list and details view must be a complete alternative to map markers.
- Use the supplied `parks.sample.json`; document any extension of data or schema in the README.
- Unredacted AI conversation logs from every tool used are part of the submission: the developer manually exports the Claude Code transcript before each fresh story context, and exports the Claude Design transcript; the generated designs are committed in `design/`.
- Visual design follows `design/README.md` and `design/screens/`; where they conflict with this SPEC, this SPEC wins.

## Non-goals

- Backend, accounts, hosted deployment.
- Search, filters, current-location support — only if the core is done with time remaining.
- Editing or contributing park data.

## Success signal

- On a phone-width viewport and on desktop, a resident can open any of the 12 sample parks from the list using only the keyboard, and from a map marker by click/tap, and see its details with missing fields handled cleanly; the interviewer runs it from the README with one install and one start command.

## Assumptions

- Municipality is the NYC-area set implied by the sample coordinates; the app is titled generically ("Find a Park").
- Parks without valid lat/lng still appear in the list and details, just without a marker.
- A data-load failure shows an error message, not a blank screen.
# Park data

Source: `requirements/parks.sample.json` — an array of 12 park objects.

## Shape

| Field | Type | May be missing/null/empty |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | string (slug) | treat as required |
| `name` | string | treat as required |
| `description` | string | yes |
| `location.lat`, `location.lng` | number | yes (no marker if absent) |
| `location.address` | string | yes |
| `amenities` | string[] (kebab-case tokens, e.g. `dog-run`) | yes / empty |
| `hours` | free-text string (e.g. `Dawn to dusk`, `24 hours`) | yes |
| `images` | string[] URLs | yes / empty |
| `acreage` | number | yes |
| `rating` | number (0–5) | yes |

Amenity tokens display as readable labels (`dog-run` → "Dog run"); unknown tokens fall back to the token humanized, never dropped.

## Image URL transformation

`parks.sample.json` is never edited. At load time each image URL is mapped by filename stem to a bundled asset: `https://images.example.com/prospect-1.jpg` → `src/assets/park-images/prospect-1.png` (source art: `design/park-images/`). A URL with no matching asset passes through unchanged, so the `onError` fallback still applies. All 12 sample URLs have a match. Describe this transformation in the README.

## Known gaps in the sample (test fixtures)

| Park | Gap |
|---|---|
| `old-mill-botanical-garden` | `description: null` |
| `cedar-hill-nature-preserve` | `images: []`, `rating: null` |
| `east-ridge-trailhead` | `images: []` |
| `highland-dog-park` | `location.address` missing |
| all | image URLs are `images.example.com` placeholders that do not resolve; the transformation above maps them to bundled art, and an unmapped URL must still fall back cleanly |
# Stack

All choices confirmed by the user.

| Concern | Choice | Why |
|---|---|---|
| UI | React | User decision. |
| Build tool | Vite | Fast scaffold; one `npm install` + `npm run dev`. |
| Styling | Tailwind CSS, mobile-first | User decision; responsive by default. |
| Map | `react-leaflet` + Leaflet | User decision; no API key. |
| Tiles | OpenStreetMap standard tiles, with attribution | Keyless; satisfies the no-credentials constraint. |
| Data | Static import of `parks.sample.json` (copied into the app) | No backend. |
| Routing | Path-based `/parks/:id` for park details; `/` is the list | Linkable details, working Back. Supersedes the `?park=` option in `design/README.md`. |
| Tests | Vitest + React Testing Library | Cover every known gap in `park-data.md` (null description, empty images, null rating, missing address, failing image URLs) plus the keyboard list→details flow. |

## Conventions

- Map initial view fits the bounds of parks that have coordinates; never depends on geolocation.
- Leaflet's default marker icons need explicit asset imports under Vite, or markers render as broken images.
- Visual design, tokens, missing-data display copy, and accessibility rules come from `design/README.md` (adopted companion) and `design/screens/`.
- Park images: pixel art lives in `src/assets/park-images/` (moved from `design/park-images/`) and is resolved via the load-time URL transformation in `park-data.md`; the image-failure fallback is still required.

  summary: Add a park-to-park navigation test (open park A, fail its image, go straight to park B) that asserts B's image renders and B's heading takes focus.
  evidence: Removing `key={park.id}` from `PhotoArea` or changing the heading-focus effect deps to `[]` passes all tests. Unreachable until Story 1.4 adds map selection that switches parks without passing through `/`.

### Story 1.6: Tests and submission README

As the interviewer,
I want evidence the app was checked and a README I can run and judge from,
So that I can evaluate usefulness, decisions, and verification.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Vitest + React Testing Library
**When** `npm test` runs
**Then** tests pass that cover every known gap in `park-data.md`: Old Mill's null description, Cedar Hill's empty images and null rating, East Ridge's empty images, Highland's missing address, an image that fails to load, and an unmatched image URL
**And** a test covers the keyboard flow: Tab to a list card, Enter, the details heading receives focus, and "All parks" returns focus to the originating card
**And** a test covers the image URL transformation without mutating the source data

**Given** the README
**When** the interviewer reads it
**Then** it contains run instructions, what works, what was left out, decisions (stack, routing, map tiles), assumptions from SPEC.md, data changes (the image URL transformation; the JSON file is unchanged), known issues (including Cedar Hill's placeholder-looking coordinates and the OSM tile usage policy), how the result was checked, approximate time spent, and next steps before public use
**And** it says where the unredacted Claude Code and Claude Design transcripts and the design files live in the repo

== _bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-1-1-scaffold-app-and-park-data-layer.md
## Implementation Notes

- vitest 4.1 + jsdom 29 instead of vitest 5 + jsdom 30: the latter declare engines that exclude Node 25 (odd release), so plain `npm install` on Node 25 picks v4/v29 and installs warning-free. Upgrade when on Node 24 LTS or 26.
- Park PNGs are under Vite's 4 KB inline limit, so `?url` yields data URIs in the production build (bundled, no separate files).
- Tests read the fixture with `process.cwd()` because jsdom gives `import.meta.url` an http scheme.
- Normalization also treats empty/whitespace strings as `null` and out-of-range `acreage` (< 0) as `null`; non-object entries are skipped.

## Design Notes

The park `number` is assigned once at load time and shared by list cards and map markers (Stories 1.2 and 1.4), so it must not be recomputed per view. `rating` stays `null` when it is not a finite number in 0–5, so the UI can never show 0 stars by accident.

## Implementation Notes

- `ParkList` also takes `onFocusUsed`, which the parent uses to clear the remembered id after the list consumes it.
- The number badge is `aria-hidden`, with an `sr-only` "N." in its place. Literal spaces sit between the card's text parts, so the accessible name reads "1. Cedar Hill Nature Preserve Cedar Hill Rd Trails …" and not run-together words.
- Decorative chevron per card (`aria-hidden`), from the phone design.
- The details route records the id only for a park that exists.

## Design Notes

Focus return has to work with browser Back, which carries no router state, so the id of the park the user came from lives in App state rather than in `location.state`. The details route records the id on mount. The list reads it once on mount, focuses that card, then clears it, so a later fresh visit to `/` does not steal focus. Story 1.3's "All parks" control will rely on the same mechanism (epic readiness decision).

The card's accessible name is all of its text (number, name, address, tags). That is acceptable for one link per card and matches the design's single-element card.

- `npm test` -- expected: all tests pass.
- `npm run build` -- expected: succeeds with no errors.

**Manual checks (if no CLI):**
- `npm run dev`, Tab through the list: amber ring on each card; Enter opens details; browser Back puts focus back on that card.

- `false` — Implementation Notes / Spec Change Log are empty (BH): the fix would edit the spec under review. The applied patches show in the diff and the triage log.
- `false` — `osmUrl` could emit `NaN` in the href (BH, EC, AA): the normalizer sets `hasCoords` only when `lat`/`lng` pass `numberInRange`, so the link never renders with non-finite coordinates.
- `low` — Null `name` gives an empty `<h1>` and alt "Photo of null", which the no-null test misses (EC, BH, AA): all 12 sample parks have names, `name` is required by the data contract, and the fix adds a fallback branch. This is the same call as 1.1 EC2, 1.2 BH1 and 1.3 pass-1 EC1/EC4.
- `low` — `0` acres / `0.0 / 5` rendered as real values (BH): the normalizer treats 0 as valid, no sample park has 0, and the design's "do not show 0" applies only to `null`.
- `false` — No range check on rating (BH): `normalizePark` already nulls ratings outside 0–5.
- `false` — Every address is muted, so the missing-address test is weak (BH): the design table asks only for "secondary text colour", and the design shows every address muted. This is the same call as pass-1 BH2.
- `false` — Deep link to `/parks/:id` moves focus to the `<h1>` (BH): focusing the page heading on route load is the intended SPA pattern, so nothing bad happens.
- `false` — The deferred-work entry lacks an id/status (BH): the ledger is new, and the loop sweep's `--migrate` handles pre-DW-format entries.
- `false` — "Photo 1 of N" implies a gallery (BH): `design/README.md` asks for exactly this label on a single image.
- `low` — A dash-only amenity token (`"-"`) renders an empty tag (EC): no sample data does this, and the fix adds a filter.
- `false` — Same park id re-rendered with new images keeps a stale failed state (EC): parks load once, and no path changes a park's images in place.
- `low` — `formatAcres` has no rounding or thousands separator (EC): sample acreages are integers ≤ 640. This is the same call as pass-1 EC3/BH4.
- `low` — `toFixed(1)` rounds a two-decimal rating (AA): no sample rating has two decimals, and one decimal is a spec Decision.
- `false` — Park-to-park refocus and `PhotoArea` reset are untested (AA, VG other): this is already deferred in `deferred-work.md` (VG2), so it is not a new finding.

## Design Notes

Focus-to-heading uses a ref plus `useEffect` keyed on `park.id`, so moving from one park to another also refocuses. The heading needs `tabIndex={-1}` to be focusable from code, and it gets no `focus-ring`, since a programmatic focus target is not a Tab stop. `PhotoArea` keeps a `failed` state and is keyed by park id, so a failure on one park does not hide another park's photo.

Missing-value tile golden example:

```jsx
<div className={missing ? 'border-dashed border-missing-line bg-missing-bg' : 'border-line bg-surface'}>
  <dt className="text-sm text-muted">Rating</dt>
  <dd className={missing ? 'italic text-muted' : 'font-semibold'}>{value ?? fallback}</dd>
</div>
```

- `npm test` -- expected: all tests pass.
- `npm run build` -- expected: succeeds with no errors.
== _bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-1-4-map-of-parks-synced-with-selection.md
## Implementation Notes

- Marker focus return after "Close park preview" is driven by a `focusId`/`onFocused` pair on `ParkMap`: closing changes the highlight, react-leaflet then swaps the marker's divIcon (replacing its DOM), so the map focuses the new button in an effect that runs after the markers' own effects. Icons are memoised per park and state so unrelated renders never replace marker DOM.
- `ParkMap` accepts `isDesktop` per the contract but renders identically at every width; App decides what activation does.
- The preview is a `<section tabIndex=-1 aria-labelledby>` overlaid at the bottom of the map (`z-[1001]`, above Leaflet controls; `bottom-8` keeps the attribution visible). Escape also closes it.
- Map wrapper is `relative isolate h-[480px]` inside the section; the section lost `flex-1` (layout is otherwise unchanged; 1.5 restyles).
- Existing details tests used `document.querySelector('img')`; OSM tile `<img>`s now exist, so those queries are scoped to the details `<article>`.

## Spec Change Log

## Review Triage Log

|---|---------|---------|----------|-------|

| BH3 | `isDesktop` passed to `ParkMap` but unused | low | Real, but the prop is in the spec's task signature; removing it edits the spec, and it causes no runtime harm | reject |
| BH5 | Map never pans to the selected park; bounds not recomputed | false | "Panning the map on selection" is a frozen Never; parks data does not change after `ready` | reject (out of scope) |
| BH6b | Second-marker swap and Back-clears-preview untested | low | Back/navigation clearing is covered by the "View park details" test via the same `location.key` effect; swap is a `key` remount | reject (unlikely) |
| BH7 | Focus effect lists unused `selectedId`; `onFocused` runs when no button found | low | Extra dep only re-runs an early-returning effect; the closed preview's park always has a marker (preview only opens from one) | reject |
| BH8/EC6 | `mql.addEventListener` missing in Safari < 14 | low | Safari 13 (2019) is far outside the target; a fallback adds a branch | reject (unlikely) |
| BH9 | Map lost `flex-1`, fixed 480px height | low | Spec task requires an explicit height; Design Notes leave layout to Story 1.5 | reject (by design) |
| BH10 | No sr "Address:" prefix; amber small text contrast unchecked | false | Amber `#B45309` on white is ≈ 5.0:1, above 4.5:1; address needs no label to be understood | reject |
| BH11 | Review diff omits lockfile, spec and sprint-status | false | Deliberate: the spec is the claims file; `npm install` and the build resolved `react-leaflet@5` against React 19 cleanly | reject |
| EC1 | Null or duplicate park `id` collides keys / `/parks/null` | low | `id` is required; sample ids unique; same call as 1.1 EC3 and 1.2 BH2 | reject (unlikely) |
| EC2 | Ids with `/ ? # %` need `encodeURIComponent` | low | All sample ids are kebab-case; same call as 1.2 | reject (unlikely) |
| EC4 | Phone: re-tapping the open preview's marker leaves focus on the marker | low | Real, but the preview is already visible; refocusing needs a counter/key bump | reject (unlikely) |
| EC7 | Null park name → empty preview `h2` | low | `name` required; all 12 have one; same call as 1.1 EC2 | reject (unlikely) |

## Design Notes

**Marker as divIcon `<button>`:** a native button gives the name and Enter/Space for free, and Leaflet fires the marker `click` from the bubbling DOM click. `keyboard: false` keeps the wrapper div out of the Tab order. No default icon means no broken Vite icon paths.

**Layout before 1.5:** the map stays stacked under the aside at every width. Story 1.5 adds the sidebar and the List/Map toggle. Phone vs desktop behaviour is decided by viewport, not by layout, so 1.5 only restyles.

**Story 1.3 is merged:** `/parks/:id` renders `ParkDetailsRoute` → `ParkDetails`, which focuses its h1 on mount. A desktop marker activation therefore lands focus on the details heading.

## Verification

**Commands:**
- `npm test` -- expected: all tests pass.
- `npm run build` -- expected: succeeds with no errors.

**Manual checks (if no CLI):**
- `npm run dev`: tiles, attribution and 12 markers show; desktop click opens details with an amber marker; at 375px a tap shows the preview; no location prompt.

# Find a Park: design handoff

UI designs for the Find a Park take-home. They were drawn on a Claude design canvas before any code was written, and they are the visual spec for the React + Tailwind + Leaflet build. The full AI transcript of the design session is in the repo alongside this folder.

**These are reference designs, not code.** Build with React, Tailwind and react-leaflet (no component library), and use the screenshots plus the rules below. Where the brief and these designs disagree, the brief wins.

## Contents

| File | What it shows |
|---|---|
| `screens/01-desktop-list-map.png` | Desktop default: park list on the left, map on the right |
| `screens/02-desktop-park-selected.png` | Desktop with a park selected: details replace the list, the marker is highlighted |
| `screens/03-desktop-missing-data.png` | Desktop details for a park with missing data (Cedar Hill: no photos, no rating) |
| `screens/04-phone-list.png` | Phone, List tab |
| `screens/05-phone-map-selected.png` | Phone, Map tab, with a park selected (preview card) |
| `screens/06-phone-details.png` | Phone details, full data (Prospect Park) |
| `screens/07-phone-missing-data.png` | Phone details, missing data (Cedar Hill) |
| `source/*.dc.html` | Canvas source files, for reference only. They need the canvas runtime and are **not** meant to run in the app. |

Screenshots are at 2x: desktop at 1280×800, phone at 390×844. The map in every screenshot is a drawn stand-in. The real app uses Leaflet with OpenStreetMap tiles.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
merge_session_transcripts.py

Collect the main transcript and every subagent transcript for a Claude Code
session ID, then interleave them into one timeline sorted by timestamp.

Claude Code stores sessions under ~/.claude/projects/<project-slug>/:
    <session-id>.jsonl                       main conversation
    <session-id>/subagents/agent-*.jsonl     each subagent's full transcript
    <session-id>/**/*.jsonl                  any other agent transcripts (picked up too)

Usage:
    python3 merge_session_transcripts.py <session-id>
    python3 merge_session_transcripts.py <session-id> -f md -o session.md
    python3 merge_session_transcripts.py <session-id> -f jsonl -o merged.jsonl
    python3 merge_session_transcripts.py <session-id> --projects-dir /path/to/.claude/projects

Formats:
    text   readable plain text (default)
    md     Markdown, one heading per message
    jsonl  the original JSON entries, merged, each tagged with "_source"
"""

import argparse
import json
import sys
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from pathlib import Path

MIN_TS = datetime.min.replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)

    mains = sorted(projects_dir.glob(f"*/{session_id}.jsonl"))

import { defineConfig } from 'vite'
import react from '@vitejs/plugin-react'
import tailwindcss from '@tailwindcss/vite'

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [react(), tailwindcss()],
  test: {
    environment: 'jsdom',
    setupFiles: ['./src/test/setup.js'],
  },
})
import { useCallback, useEffect, useState } from 'react'
import { loadParks } from './parks.js'

/** Loads parks once; `retry` refetches after an error. */
export function useParks() {
  const [attempt, setAttempt] = useState(0)
  const [state, setState] = useState({ status: 'loading', parks: [] })

  useEffect(() => {
    let cancelled = false
    setState({ status: 'loading', parks: [] })
    loadParks().then(
      (parks) => {
        if (!cancelled) setState({ status: 'ready', parks })
      },
      () => {
        if (!cancelled) setState({ status: 'error', parks: [] })
      },
    )
    return () => {
      cancelled = true
    }
  }, [attempt])

  const retry = useCallback(() => setAttempt((n) => n + 1), [])

  return { status: state.status, parks: state.parks, retry }
}

import { useCallback, useEffect, useState } from 'react'
import { Link, Route, Routes, useLocation, useMatch, useNavigate, useParams } from 'react-router'
import ParkDetails from './components/ParkDetails.jsx'
import ParkList from './components/ParkList.jsx'
import ParkMap from './components/ParkMap.jsx'
import ParkPreview from './components/ParkPreview.jsx'
import { useIsDesktop } from './data/useIsDesktop.js'
import { useParks } from './data/useParks.js'

const cardClass = 'rounded-[10px] border border-line bg-surface p-4'
const linkClass = 'focus-ring rounded text-forest underline'

function NotFound() {
  return (
    <div>
      <h1 className="text-lg font-bold">Park not found</h1>
      <p className="mt-2">
        <Link to="/" className={linkClass}>
          Back to all parks
        </Link>
      </p>
    </div>
  )
}

function ParkDetailsRoute({ parks, onOpen }) {
  const { id } = useParams()
  const park = parks.find((p) => p.id === id)

  // Record the opened park so the list can return focus to its card.
  useEffect(() => {
    if (park) onOpen(park.id)
  }, [park, onOpen])

  if (!park) return <NotFound />

  return <ParkDetails park={park} />
}

function ParksPanel({ status, parks, retry, lastParkId, setLastParkId }) {
  const clearLastParkId = useCallback(() => setLastParkId(null), [setLastParkId])

  if (status === 'loading') {
    return (
      <p role="status" className={cardClass}>
        Loading parks…
      </p>
    )
  }

  if (status === 'error') {
    return (
      <div role="alert" className={cardClass}>
        <p>Couldn't load parks. Try again.</p>
        <button
          type="button"
          onClick={retry}
          className="focus-ring mt-3 min-h-11 rounded-lg bg-forest px-4 font-semibold text-white hover:bg-forest-dark"
        >
          Retry
        </button>
      </div>
    )
  }

  return (
    <Routes>
      <Route
        path="/"
        element={
          <ParkList parks={parks} focusParkId={lastParkId} onFocusUsed={clearLastParkId} />
        }
      />
      <Route
        path="/parks/:id"
        element={<ParkDetailsRoute parks={parks} onOpen={setLastParkId} />}
      />
      <Route path="*" element={<NotFound />} />
    </Routes>
  )
}

export default function App() {
  const { status, parks, retry } = useParks()
  // Id of the last-opened park. Lives in App state (not location.state) so
  // focus can return to its card after browser Back, which carries no state.
  const [lastParkId, setLastParkId] = useState(null)

  const navigate = useNavigate()
  const location = useLocation()
  const isDesktop = useIsDesktop()
  const selectedId = useMatch('/parks/:id')?.params.id ?? null
  // Phone-only marker preview; cleared on any navigation and when the viewport becomes desktop.

  const clearFocusMarkerId = useCallback(() => setFocusMarkerId(null), [])

  useEffect(() => {
    setPreviewId(null)
  }, [location.key])

  useEffect(() => {
    if (isDesktop && previewId) {
      // Focus may be inside the preview: hand it back to the marker, as closing does.
      setFocusMarkerId(previewId)
      setPreviewId(null)

        // Re-activating the park already open would push a duplicate history entry.

      } else setPreviewId(id)

          <ParksPanel
            status={status}
            parks={parks}
            retry={retry}
            lastParkId={lastParkId}
            setLastParkId={setLastParkId}
          />
